# Loop journal — tool-hardening

> Per-loop append-only journal (loop-creator v1.14.0+). One entry per fire, newest at bottom.
> Schema (going-forward entries):
>
>     ## fire N · YYYY-MM-DD · skill vX.Y.Z · <commit-sha>
>     meta: value-class=micro-fix|new-capability|wiring|refactor · pkg=@muse/… · kind=… · verdict=PASS|FAIL · firesSinceDrill=N
>     ratchet: testFiles … · fabrication 0 · <eval delta>
>     - what …  - why …  - review points …  - risk …
>
> Theme: TOOL expansion & hardening. Why per-loop (not a shared digest): 4 loops run concurrently; a shared
> append file collides every fire and pollutes the version↔outcome correlation. Disjoint
> paths → no merge race, no pollution. Convention: [README](README.md).
> (Entries below the schema line were MIGRATED from the old shared loop-digest.md, original headers kept.)

## fire 1 — 2026-06-12 · Theme: TOOL expansion & hardening

- **What:** Added `groundedArgs: ["notes"]` to the `muse.tasks.update` tool (+ a unit test verifying the declaration).
- **Why:** update's free-text `notes` field was outside the anti-fabrication boundary, so an 8B model could fabricate notes the user never said and persist them to disk (tasks add/calendar were already protected). Extends the grounding edge.
- **Review points:** `packages/mcp/src/loopback-tasks.ts` (one line in the update tool def) + `packages/mcp/test/tasks-reminders-tool-schema.test.ts` (test). The gating evaluator (Opus) traced the runtime path and PASSed — `agent-runtime.ts:857-860` applies groundedArgs generically, so the declaration is sufficient.
- **Risk:** the test only verifies the *declaration* (the drop *behavior* itself is guaranteed by the shared mechanism + upstream tests). A regression in the projection wiring would not be caught by this test. `title` is intentionally left ungrounded (rename intent). Build/test (mcp 1655) · lint 0.

## fire 2 — 2026-06-12 · Theme: TOOL expansion & hardening

- **What:** Added `groundedArgs: ["relationship"]` to the `add_contact` tool (+ a unit test verifying the declaration).
- **Why:** contacts add's free-text `relationship` field ("doctor"/"manager") was outside the anti-fabrication boundary — asking to "add Bob" could let the 8B model fabricate and store a relationship. The next actuator in the tool-arg grounding item (following fire 1's tasks.update).
- **Review points:** `packages/mcp/src/contacts-tool.ts` (one line) + `packages/mcp/test/contacts-tool.test.ts`. The gating evaluator (Opus) traced a *different* registration path (a direct MuseTool, not MCP loopback) and confirmed it — `toModelTool` (tools/index:385) carries groundedArgs, and `agent-runtime:857-859` applies it. Not inert.
- **Risk:** same as fire 1 (declaration test; the drop behavior is guaranteed by the shared mechanism). `name` is required so it's ungrounded. vision-auto (`commands-ask:2573`) is a deterministic extraction path, so it's outside the threat model. mcp 1656 · lint 0.

> ⚠️ **Next fire (fire 3) = review gate.** Build paused, waiting for Jinan to read the accumulated fire 1–2 digest before merge.

## fire 3 — 2026-06-12 · Review gate + loop self-improvement

- **What:** At the fire 1–2 review gate, Jinan evaluated → pinned 4 loop weaknesses into the contract/skill as guards (§4.5): ① value-first (not "whatever is easiest to verify"; deferrals must be explicit) ② diversity (no repeating the same pattern) ③ behavioral acceptance (declaration-only tests forbidden) ④ token budget (batching + risk-tiering) ⑤ failure drills. + **ran a fault-injection drill**.
- **Why:** the first 2 fires ran the *machinery* well, but the *output* was low-ambition (same micro pattern, declaration-only tests, expensive tokens, no verification of failure paths). Needed empirical proof that the mechanism can be trusted.
- **Review points:** `loop-engineering.md §4.5` (guards) + `SKILL.md` ②③④b. **Drill evidence:** injected a deliberately inert slice (adding a `category` grounding to tasks add that doesn't exist in the schema) → the Opus gating evaluator returned **VERDICT: FAIL** (inert · no behavioral test · breaks 3 existing test axes) → rolled back with `git restore` → mcp 1656 back to green.
- **Risk:** the 4 guards are *prompt instructions*, so whether future fires actually follow them can only be confirmed live (the drill empirically confirmed ④b). The drill is one-off (repeated drills cost tokens).

## fire 5 — 2026-06-12 · Regression fix (skill v1.8.0)

- **What:** Fixed a failing test on main, `program.test.ts > pluralises 'day' (goal 129)` — replaced hardcoded dates (2026-05-13~15) with a relative date via `daysAgoIso(n)`.
- **Why:** regression-first rule (①). `muse routine`'s window is `Date.now()-30d` (commands-routine.ts:122, not injectable), so the hardcoded dates would eventually fall outside the window as time passed → 0 sessions → the "across 1 day" expectation broke. **The production pluralise code is fine** (line 147); the brittle test was the cause.
- **Review points:** only `apps/cli/test/program.test.ts` changed (only the tsIso values). Assertions unchanged. The gating evaluator (Opus) adversarially checked "is this just weakening the test to game it" → confirmed no UTC date-counting or off-by-one issues → PASS.
- **Risk:** there may be more time-bomb tests of this kind (other hardcoded dates). cli 2492 green · lint 0. (Unrelated flake: chat-grounding "fails soft" 5s timeout — passed this time, unresolved.)

## fire 6 — 2026-06-12 · Hardened the signal scout (skill v1.8.1)

- **What:** Hardened the signal scout (`run-log-analysis.ts`) so **non-answer (ungrounded) empty responses no longer count as failures** + made the script extract the answer.
- **Why:** value-first flagged the scout's #1 finding (browser-read ungrounded ×7), which turned out to be **dev noise** — empty answers (tools []) from my browser tests on 2026-06-11. If the #1 finding from a tool just built is noise, it can't be trusted → fixed the tool itself (diversity: a different KIND from fire 5).
- **Review points:** `apps/cli/src/run-log-analysis.ts` (isFailureEvent: short-circuits on success===false first, then excludes only ungrounded+empty answers) + 3 tests + `scout-signals.mjs` (answer extraction). **End-to-end proof:** re-ran against real data — 1 cluster → **0 clusters** (noise eliminated) + the "clean board → tier 2" message confirmed the 3-tier ladder works. Corrected the noise item in the backlog to Dropped.
- **Risk:** a failed run (success:false) still counts even with an empty answer (evaluator confirmed). Paraphrase merging and distinguishing real usage vs. synthetic traces are future work. cli 2495 · lint 0.

- **Why:** value-first picked mac readers (a new capability); behavior was verified via the existing injectable-runner mock (8 tests, build green). But the tool was **never wired into `actuator-tools.ts`** (the model-exposed set) = inert — the model can never select it (tool-calling.md: "if it can't be selected, it isn't delivered"). No eval:tools case either.

- **Risk:** none (rolled back). Lesson: a "new tool" slice means not just the definition but also **the wiring + eval selection case**. The gating evaluator caught a *test-green but inert* slice — the first genuine FAIL of a normal fire, proof of "a verifier you can trust."

## fire (TOOL loop) — 2026-06-12 · web_download post-redirect SSRF re-check (EXPANSION-scouted), gate PASS (skill v1.9.0)

- **What:** Closed a hole where `web_download` never re-checked SSRF against the final `response.url` after a redirect — reapplied assertPublicHttpUrl after the fetch but before writing to disk (mirroring the sibling web-read/fetch-readable-url). Behavioral test (metadata redirect → refused + not logged).
- **Why:** the TOOL backlog was thin → the 3-tier ladder's **EXPANSION gap-scout** found a genuine security gap (not busywork). A public→private redirect could reach metadata/internal hosts and get written to disk — a real SSRF.
- **Review points:** `web-download-tool.ts` (4-line re-check, before the write, fail-closed) + `.test.ts` (redirect→private case). The gating evaluator (Opus, security-grade full) confirmed ordering · fail-closed · sibling parity · happy-path · STABLE 3/3 PASS.
- **Risk/residual:** production lookup isn't wired, so sync-only (catches literal private IPs but not DNS-rebinding) — same as the existing guard; logged as a new residual item (◦) in the backlog. mcp 1668 · lint 0.

## fire (TOOL loop) — 2026-06-12 · DNS-rebinding SSRF closed (FAIL→test-fix→re-PASS), gate PASS (skill v1.9.0)

- **What:** Removed the `deps.lookup ? async : sync` bypass in web_download/web_action → always uses the async guard (defaultLookup = Node's dns resolving + checking) → the no-lookup production path is now also blocked against DNS-rebinding (a public name resolving to a private IP). Hermetic tests (injected privateLookup + dns-stub no-lookup).
- **Why:** a residual (◦) surfaced by the previous SSRF fire (value-first security). The sync guard only caught literal private IPs, leaving rebinding wide open.
- **Review points:** `web-download-tool.ts` · `web-action-tool.ts` (bypass removed) · `run-actuator-by-name.ts` (lookup wiring) + tests. **Two-stage gate:** first Opus pass caught a fake test (relied on NXDOMAIN, not actually rebinding) → FAIL → fixed the test to be hermetic (injected privateLookup + dns-stub) → second Opus pass **confirmed re-introducing the bypass makes the no-lookup test FAIL** (a genuine discriminator) → PASS.
- **Risk:** none. Production now does a real DNS lookup on every web fetch (accepted for security). mcp 1670 · lint 0. Lesson: even when production is correct, *the test must prove the OUTCOME* to pass (behavioral acceptance).

## fire (TOOL loop) — 2026-06-12 · loopback-filesystem symlink-escape closed, gate PASS (skill v1.9.0)

- **What:** the MCP filesystem server's allowlist was lexical-only, allowing a symlink escape (/allowed/x→/etc/passwd); added a realpath second gate to `checkAllowed` (both path and root realpath'd, refuse if the real path is outside root, fail-closed on throw/ENOENT) across read/list/stat.
- **Why:** an EXPANSION scout runner-up (value-first security, a different vector than SSRF = path/symlink). file_read already had a realpath guard, but the MCP variant lacked it.
- **Review points:** `loopback-filesystem.ts` (checkAllowed now an async 2-gate + injectable realpath) + 8 new tests. The gating evaluator (Opus full) confirmed the escape is blocked · boundary is precise · **not an optional-realpath hole** (production always defaults to realpath, confirmed via caller grep) · macOS /var symmetry · no regression PASS.
- **Risk:** a residual realpath→read TOCTOU window remains (common to all realpath guards, not a new regression). mcp 1678 · lint 0.
## fire (TOOL loop) — 2026-06-13 · mac_screenshot arbitrary-write closed (FAIL→fix→re-PASS), gate PASS (skill v1.9.0)

- **What:** Closed `mac_screenshot`'s unguarded `path` (arbitrary file overwrite) — allowlist (~/Desktop·Downloads·tmp) + tilde expansion + basename + parent realpath + **full-target realpath** (rejects symlink-at-target, fail-closed, runner not invoked). 6 behavioral tests.
- **Why:** the EXPANSION scout found this as "the only remaining unguarded WRITE path" (highest severity, a destructive write). The last item in the security sweep.
- **Review points:** `macos-tools.ts` (resolveScreenshotPath + injectable realpath) + 6 tests. **Two-stage gate:** the first Opus pass caught a *silent* symlink-at-target residual (re-introducing a class the previous fire 77004a6f closed) → FAIL → closed via full-target realpath → the second Opus pass confirmed discrimination · no regression PASS.
- **Risk:** a residual realpath→write TOCTOU window remains (common to all realpath guards). macos 83 · lint 0. Security sweep (SSRF×2 · symlink · write) complete → next fire should be a different KIND.

The 2 regressions had to be cleaned up before committing since pnpm check was red (quick self-eval doesn't catch it) — regression-first principle.

## [TOOL loop] fire 2 (v1.10.0, cron 23eff34a) — 2026-06-13 · Theme: TOOL expansion & hardening

- **What:** Introduced quarantine-on-corrupt for @muse/calendar's two file stores (LocalCalendarProvider, FileCalendarCredentialStore) — instead of silently emptying a corrupt file, it's now preserved as `<file>.corrupt-<ts>`. One shared helper, corrupt-quarantine.ts, called from 4 corrupt branches.
- **Why:** a corrupt (parse-failure/schema-mismatch) read returns an empty result → the next atomic write permanently overwrites the corrupt-but-recoverable original = data loss. The sibling reminders-store already quarantines. Writes were already atomic, so quarantine was the only missing piece.
- **Review points:** corrupt-quarantine.ts (new helper) + local-provider.ts readAll (parse catch + non-array events branch) + credential-store.ts readAll (schema mismatch + catch). TDD 3 tests RED 3/3 → GREEN, calendar 152, check 0, lint 0. Fable-5 evaluator PASS (ENOENT/transient IO isn't quarantined, the predicate is invariant = strictly safer, rename preserves 0600, concurrency-safe). Along the way, removed a raw NUL (backlog.md:63) left by fire-1's backlog write-back — a byte-hygiene regression.
- **Risk:** local-provider's per-entry isPersistedEvent flatMap still silently drops *individual* corrupt events (partial loss, no logging) — logged in the backlog as a separate out-of-scope slice. `.corrupt-*` files are not GC'd (recovery material, by design). No grounding-floor impact (local disk integrity, model/egress/gate unchanged).
## [TOOL loop] fire 3 (v1.10.0, cron 23eff34a) — 2026-06-13 · Theme: TOOL expansion & hardening

- **What:** Fixed a missing embedder migration on the chat path — refreshStaleNotesIndexForChat early-returned on CONTENT changes only, so chat-only users (desktop doesn't call ask) never migrated their legacy v1 index, leaving it permanently mismatched with v2-moe queries. Model mismatch is now also a staleness trigger.
- **Why:** matching v2-moe query vectors against a v1 index = cross-model cosine noise floats above the 0.5 authoritative floor, degrading recall quality (a grounding-quality bug). ask already re-embeds, but chat didn't.
- **Review points:** chat-grounding.ts — a pure helper notesIndexNeedsModelMigration (resolveIndexModel(existing,req)!=existing) + refreshStaleNotesIndexForChat exported and turned DI (reads the model before the staleness gate, re-embeds on modelStale||contentStale). TDD 5 tests (1 helper unit + 4 DI behavioral: legacy-fresh→re-embeds to default, default/custom-fresh→doesn't, content-stale→still does) RED→GREEN. cli 2525, check 0, lint 0. Fable-5 evaluator PASS (no per-turn loop · production wiring live · fail-soft).
- **Risk:** if the embedder is DOWN during a model-mismatch rebuild, reindexNotes can drop the prior-entry carry-forward → could save an empty index (fail-close: zero hits→refusal, not fabrication; an existing path). Logged as a separate slice in the backlog. No grounding-floor impact (indexing path, gate logic unchanged).


## [TOOL loop] fire 4 (v1.10.0, cron 23eff34a) — 2026-06-13 · Theme: TOOL expansion & hardening

- **What:** Fixed prototype pollution in muse.json.merge's deepMerge — an own "__proto__" key in model args (from JSON.parse) hit Object.prototype's setter via result["__proto__"]=, replacing result's prototype + injecting inherited fields (e.g. isAdmin) + losing keys. Special-cased only __proto__: read the existing value via getOwnPropertyDescriptor, deepMerge, then write it back as an own data prop via defineProperty.
- **Why:** a real prototype-pollution vector in a model-facing tool handler. The signal scout was clean (0 clusters), so found via a tier-2 codebase EXPANSION scout (Fable-5). ## [TOOL loop] fire 5 (v1.10.0, cron 23eff34a) — 2026-06-13 · Theme: TOOL expansion & hardening

- **What:** Fixed a symlink-contract violation in muse.fs.stat — the description promises "doesn't follow symlinks, reports kind=symlink" but it used fsLib.stat (which follows), so it could never actually report a symlink. Added an optional lstat to the fs seam + wired a default impl + the tool now uses (fsLib.lstat ?? fsLib.stat).
- **Why:** a model-facing tool's documented contract was unmeetable (a symlink was always reported as the target's kind). Runner-up from fire-4's EXPANSION scout. Codebase gap, since the signal board was clean.
- **Review points:** loopback-filesystem.ts (lstat seam + default + one line in the stat tool) + loopback-filesystem.test.ts (lstat→isSymbolicLink → kind=symlink vs. stat-follow → file) TDD 1 RED→GREEN. mcp 1680, check 0, lint 0. Fable-5 evaluator PASS (reproduced RED via a HEAD sandbox compile · realpath escape guard intact · read/list unchanged). Because lstat runs on the lexical path, the escape guard already runs before stat.
- **Risk:** read/list still follow symlinks on the lexical path (by design; the realpath guard blocks escape but a symlink-swap TOCTOU window remains → a separate slice in the backlog). runner-up atomicWriteFile tmp leak still OPEN. No grounding-floor impact (fs metadata tool, gate unchanged).


## [TOOL loop] fire 6 (v1.10.0, cron 23eff34a) — 2026-06-13 · Theme: TOOL expansion & hardening

- **What:** Fixed a tmp leak in atomicWriteFile (the shared sidecar-store write primitive) — if open→write→rename fails at any point, a `<file>.tmp-<pid>-<uuid>` was orphaned in every sidecar directory (memory/tasks/reminders/action-log/…), accumulating litter. Wrapped open→write→rename→chmod in try/catch: on failure, fs.rm(tmp,{force}) then rethrow the original error.
- **Why:** a resource leak (disk litter) — the last runner-up from fire-4/5's EXPANSION scout. Codebase gap, since the signal board was clean.
- **Review points:** atomic-file-store.ts (try/catch + rm) + atomic-file-store.test.ts (target=directory→rename throws→rejection AND 0 .tmp- files). TDD 1 RED→GREEN. mcp 1681, check 0, lint 0. Fable-5 evaluator PASS (reproduced RED via a HEAD source swap · original error not swallowed · UUID means no cross-writer race). Non-blocking note: if close() throws inside finally it can mask the writeFile error (an existing JS semantics quirk, unrelated to this fix).
- **Risk:** near zero — happy path unchanged, rm only ever targets this call's own tmp (UUID), and force means it's a no-op if open failed. No grounding-floor impact (file IO primitive, gate unchanged).

## [TOOL loop] fire 17 (skill v1.11.2, cron 5388335b) — 2026-06-13 · Theme: TOOL expansion & hardening

- **What:** web_download buffered the entire response body via arrayBuffer() before checking the size-cap → a multi-GB/unbounded body could ignore the cap and fill RAM (memory-exhaustion DoS). Fix: a Content-Length precheck + a streaming read via getReader(), aborting via reader.cancel() and rejecting the moment the accumulated size exceeds the cap.
- **Why:** runner-up from fire-15. A different KIND (resource-exhaustion/DoS, distinct from fire15's overwrite and fire16's concurrency). Since a server can lie about or omit Content-Length, streaming abort is the real defense.
- **Review points:** web-download-tool.ts (CL precheck + getReader stream + early abort + no-body fallback) + web-download-tool.test.ts (an instrumented 20×100B stream, cap 250B → abort after ~3 chunks, nothing written) RED→GREEN. mcp 1700, check 0 (after re-running a flaky cli test), lint 0. Fable-5 evaluator PASS (under-cap downloads byte-identical · absent/garbage CL doesn't over-reject · reproduced RED via a HEAD 21 pull).
- **Risk:** near zero — under-cap downloads reassemble byte-identical via the stream, CL of 0/NaN falls through to the stream itself. RATCHET: testFiles 893 unchanged (+1 case), fabrication stays 0. Aside: 2 consecutive fires hit a flaky cli test (chat-grounding "fails soft") ⏳ logged in the backlog. No grounding-floor impact (download resource safety, gate unchanged).

## [TOOL loop] fire 30 (skill v1.11.2, cron 5388335b) — 2026-06-13 · Theme: TOOL expansion & hardening

- **What:** muse.fs read corrupted multi-byte UTF-8 at the truncation boundary — `buffer.subarray(0,maxBodyBytes).toString("utf8")` produces U+FFFD if the cap lands mid-multi-byte-sequence. The evaluator also flagged a runner-up (loopback-fetch's readBodyWithCap has the same class) → logged for helper reuse (◦).
- **Review points:** loopback-filesystem.ts (exported utf8SafeSliceEnd + wired into read) + loopback-filesystem.test.ts (6 helper unit cases: fits/Korean-mid/exact-boundary/4-byte-emoji/ASCII-unchanged/non-positive) + mcp.test.ts (e2e: fake-fs "가나다라" maxBodyBytes:8 → "가나" · no U+FFFD) RED (reverting the wiring produced "가나�") → GREEN. mcp 185 files/1735, check 0 (all packages: agent-core 1831 · cli 2535 · api 850), lint 0. Fable-5 PASS: independently reconfirmed RED, fuzzed the helper against an optimal-prefix oracle over 2000+ cases (never exceeds the cap · never over-trims a fitting char · longest valid prefix), ASCII tests green.
- **Risk:** near zero — only truncated reads go through the helper, non-truncated reads unchanged, the existing ASCII truncation test (length 50) stays green. `end` only ever decreases at maxBytes, so overshoot is structurally impossible; malformed all-continuation-bytes input terminates immediately via the end>0 guard. ## fire 55 · 2026-06-13 · skill v1.14.0 · 25913406
meta: value-class=hardening · pkg=@muse/mcp · kind=security-fix(web_action SSRF-after-redirect) · verdict=PASS · firesSinceDrill=2
ratchet: testFiles 921 unchanged (1 `it` added to the existing web-action.test.ts) · fabrication stays 0 · eval unchanged (schema/name identical)
- **What:** Closed an SSRF-after-redirect hole in `web_action` (risk:execute, model-selected) — the SSRF guard (assertPublicHttpUrl) only checked the original URL once, but fetchImpl had no `redirect` option, so the default follow could chase a 3xx to a private/loopback host (127.0.0.1 · 169.254.169.254 the cloud-metadata address), and 307/308 also re-sends the body. Fixed via `redirect:"manual"` + fail-closed handling of a 3xx (performed:false · logs the Location).
- **Why:** high-value, non-personal-data hardening (security). The scout demonstrated it asymmetrically: the READ path (fetchReadableUrl) already re-verifies the final host after a redirect, but the riskier WRITE path was missing this. No RATCHET pressure · KIND=security-fix (escapes the personal-data-wrap fires). Like math_eval, the scout double-checked whether this was possibly not-a-bug by actually reading the handler+tests → confirmed a real defect.
- **Review points:** web-action.ts (fetch init `redirect:"manual"` + a fail-closed 3xx branch after checking response.ok + logging, covers 307/308 via >=300&&<400) + web-action.test.ts (+1 behavioral: a fake fetch mimicking real behavior—follows→200 unless manual; RED demonstrated performed:true = SSRF succeeded → GREEN performed:false + `init.redirect==="manual"` + logged). mcp 1811 (2xx/429/5xx/deny/timeout contracts unaffected), check 0, lint 0. Opus PASS 6/6: the fix is real (redirect:manual mechanism) · behavioral RED→GREEN · no collateral (the branch runs after .ok) · strengthens the floor · the opaqueredirect edge case also fails closed.
- **Risk:** none — strengthens the floor (outbound-safety SSRF), risk:execute unchanged · approval still runs first, normal 2xx/429/5xx paths unchanged (only a new 3xx branch), a 3xx isn't a false failure (no redirect was applied). A concurrent loop caused worktree churn (my test was committed first, the fix landed in a separate commit 25913406 — reconfirmed HEAD test+fix are both green). MUSE_LOCAL_ONLY · fabrication · banking unaffected.

## fire 57 · 2026-06-13 · skill v1.14.0 · (sweep attempted → REVERTED, finding recorded)
meta: value-class=revert+finding · pkg=apps/cli grounding batteries · kind=unsafe-sweep-reverted · verdict=REVERTED · firesSinceDrill=3
ratchet: testFiles unchanged (guard removed) · fabrication stays 0 · eval unchanged
- **What:** As a fire-56 follow-up, attempted the slice "13 grounding/eval batteries hardcode the legacy `nomic-embed-text` → align all of them with production's `DEFAULT_EMBED_MODEL` (v2-moe) + a guard test" — but switching to v2-moe made **cited-recall FAIL 2/6** (an in-corpus "home insurance renew?" cosine of 0.546 → dropped from confident to ambiguous), so it was **unsafe and ROLLED BACK** (restored the legacy setting in 7 scripts + removed the guard).
- **Why:** ★A finding — fire 56 (faithfulness-rate) goes through the RGV rubric path, so v2-moe was safe there, but **the cosine-threshold batteries (cited-recall, etc.) have `classifyRetrievalConfidence` thresholds calibrated against the old nomic-embed-text's cosine distribution**. v2-moe's absolute cosines run lower (relative ranking is excellent, KO hit@1 100%), so borderline in-corpus cases fall from confident to ambiguous. In other words, "just swap the embedder" isn't enough — **the thresholds need recalibration**. A concurrent loop had swept my uncommitted change into HEAD, landing the broken batteries on main → this revert un-breaks it.
- **Review points:** the revert = restored DEFAULT_EMBED_MODEL→legacy across 7 grounding batteries (grounding-delta(-squad) · cited-recall · rubric-gate · chat-grounding-rate · proactive-recall-gate · council-self-abstention) + removed grounding-embedder-guard.test.mjs. All 7 pass `node --check`. faithfulness-rate (fire 56) kept v2-moe (verified safe).
- **Risk/finding (grounding domain):** ★PRODUCTION IMPLICATION — if production ships v2-moe with the same `classifyRetrievalConfidence` thresholds, it could over-refuse a borderline in-corpus question ("home insurance renew?") — the source retrieves at rank 1, but the cosine falls below the confident threshold. The cited-recall case suggests exactly that. The grounding loop/Jinan should review recalibrating the confidence thresholds for v2-moe (out of scope for this deep-fix under the tool-hardening theme; recorded honestly here).

meta: value-class=hardening · pkg=@muse/mcp · kind=no-partial-side-effect(notes.append precheck-before-write) · verdict=PASS · firesSinceDrill=8
ratchet: testFiles +1 case (mcp.test notes append over-cap) · fabrication stays 0 · eval unaffected (handler correctness)
- **What:** muse.notes append's handler **wrote first** then checked the cap via stat, so an over-cap append error still left oversized bytes on disk (a partial side-effect). Now checks the resulting size (current bytes + Buffer.byteLength of the append) **before writing** → rejects and doesn't write if it would exceed the cap. Exact-cap is still allowed (`> maxFileBytes`, matching save).
- **Why:** a violation of agent-testing.md's "a failed action changes nothing." Worse, since the read handler rejects an oversized file, a "failed" append made the note **unreadable** = a data-integrity issue, not cosmetic. The Opus scout had judged this edge as marginal; reframed it as no-partial-side-effect + read-failure and reassessed it as a genuine fix.
- **Review points:** loopback-notes.ts's append handler (checks currentBytes from stat + appendBytes together, before mkdir+appendFile) + mcp.test (seeded 601 bytes, attempted a 600-byte append → error + a re-read shows UNCHANGED, RED→GREEN). lint clean (comment-only catch). pnpm check's auth flake (isolated 61 green). Opus judge PASS 5/5 (confirmed no-partial-write via read-back, no off-by-one, "marginal undersells it").
- **Risk:** none — the rejection path mutates nothing, normal/first-file/exact-cap behavior preserved, path-sandbox still runs first. [Recovery note: this entry's fire 80 ⑤b commit was lost in concurrent-loop merge churn, restored at fire 81]

Lesson: a fail-close guard must check for a concrete value, not key presence (empty bypasses it). ## fire 105 · 2026-06-14 · skill v1.14.0 · da9ddba0
meta: value-class=hardening(a reachable SSRF security fix) · pkg=@muse/mcp · kind=SSRF bypass closed(IPv4-mapped IPv6 hex normalization) · verdict=PASS · firesSinceDrill=3

- **What:** web-url-guard.ts's `isPrivateIPv6` only matched the **dotted** IPv4-mapped form (`::ffff:127.0.0.1`) → WHATWG's `new URL()` actually normalizes the host to **hex** (`::ffff:7f00:1`), so the dotted branch never fires on a real URL → loopback/cloud-metadata (`169.254.169.254`→`::ffff:a9fe:a9fe`)/mapped RFC-1918 addresses pass as "public" = a **reachable SSRF/metadata-leak hole** in the wired web_download/web_action/web_read tools. Fix: decode the hex's 2 groups into an octet (`(hi>>8)&0xff…`) and re-run isPrivateIPv4; a normal public mapped address (`::ffff:8.8.8.8`) still passes.
- **Why:** forces non-autoconfigure (ratchet was at 5/8 autoconfigure) + a different KIND (security, escaping 3 consecutive event-render fires). The scout reviewed file-read (already realpath-reverified) · web-download (size/redirect capped) · smart-home (fail-close) and picked this as the only reachable security defect. Directly reproduced the exploit with a compile-time guard (ALLOWED ✗) → fixed → blocked ✓. Security work is exactly this theme's core value.
- **Review points:** web-url-guard.ts (a hexMapped regex + octet decoding, preserving the existing dotted branch) + 2 cases added to the existing boundaries test (a helper hex-form test + assertPublicHttpUrlSync end-to-end for 3 URLs). Confirmed the existing dotted cases (line 49-52) only exercised inputs that happened to match the regex, **never actually covering the real URL path = a false sense of confidence** (now filled by the hex-path cases). 1864 green, isolated @muse/mcp reconfirmed. ④b Opus judge PASS 7/7 — closely verified the octet decoding arithmetic (a9fe→169.254.169.254) · padded/uncompressed forms normalize identically · no over-matching (`2001:db8::ffff:1` isn't incorrectly blocked). ⚠️ pnpm check exit-134 was a @muse/auth OOM (concurrent-loop memory saturation); auth isolated 61/61 green · the mcp change has no dependency on auth → environmental, not a real regression.
- **Risk:** none — only makes the guard stricter (confirmed no over-blocking: 8.8.8.8/example.com/GUA all pass), a purely additive classification function (+10 lines, existing clauses unchanged), a read-path only. Non-blocking follow-up: the deprecated IPv4-compatible form (`::127.0.0.1`, no ffff) isn't decoded — logged as a backlog ◦ (low-risk, an RFC4291-deprecated form).

## fire 108 · 2026-06-14 · skill v1.14.0 · 106b520c
meta: value-class=hardening(a reachable SSRF completeness fix) · pkg=@muse/mcp · kind=deprecated IPv4-compatible + SIIT IPv6 SSRF form closed · verdict=PASS · firesSinceDrill=6
ratchet: testFiles 990 held (+2 cases to the existing web-url-guard-boundaries.test.ts) · fabrication stays 0 · eval unaffected (a deterministic guard)
- **What:** ① at baseline, a lint:pass→fail regression appeared, but diagnosis traced it to **an uncommitted concurrent cognition loop's followup-capture-hook.ts:217** (committed main was clean — stashing just that file removed the error), not my responsibility → proceeded normally. fire-105 had only closed the IPv4-mapped form, but a probe **confirmed the deprecated IPv4-compatible form still bypasses it**: `new URL()` normalizes `[::127.0.0.1]`→`::7f00:1`, `[::169.254.169.254]`→`::a9fe:a9fe` (the metadata address is REACHABLE), `[::ffff:0:127.0.0.1]`→`::ffff:0:7f00:1` (SIIT) — all classified as public. Fix: replaced the single mapped regex with a general `expandIPv6Hextets` parser + an embedded-IPv4 check (if the upper 6 hextets are all 0x0000/0xffff, decode the low 32 bits as IPv4 → isPrivateIPv4).

- **Risk:** none — try wraps only generate+parse (the decision branch is outside, normal allow/block unaffected), every error path fails closed (no allowed:true path), a pure deterministic function. - **Review points:** pre-verified 18 cases in node → RED (em-dash/colon hedges + a KO continuation) → implemented (the seam + the negation skip) → recall's 40 files + the cli's 2 refusal/sufficiency files green · lint clean. ④b Opus judge (maximum scrutiny given the grounding floor) PASS 5/5 — #2 (independently node-verified the fire-139 regression across 9 cases: 5 pure cases all true · 4 hedge cases all false, all correct) · #1 (em-dash/colon hedges RED→GREEN) · #3 (**honestly identified a residual**: a negation-containing fabrication is a slip=a narrow unsafe-direction gap, but the common positive form is caught and the KO false-positive is fixed, net-positive) · #4 (no regression across the 14 advisory sites) · #5 (no throw, NEGATION_RE isn't global).
- **Risk:** low — net-positive (catching common hedges + fixing a false-positive outweighs missing a rare negated hedge). ## fire 143 · 2026-06-14 · skill v1.14.0 · 2fe2b256
meta: value-class=correctness-bug(memory cross-namespace data loss) · pkg=@muse/memory · kind=forget now takes a namespace-scoped delete(a kind argument) · verdict=PASS · firesSinceDrill=5
ratchet: testFiles 1021→1021(memory-operation.test +2, file-store.test +1) · fabrication strengthened ("tell it everything" core data integrity) · eval unaffected (a pure store logic change) · package diversity apps/cli→@muse/memory (a fresh surface, maximum diversification)
- **What:** the auto-extractor's DELETE branch didn't pass the in-scope `kind` (fact/preference) through, calling `forget(userId, key)` → `forget` deletes from **both** the facts and preferences stores. Since fact/preference frequently share the same normalized key (pet/city/name…), and auto-extract runs every turn, a FACT retraction ("no more pet") silently deleted a PREFERENCE the user never retracted ("prefers dogs"). Added an optional `kind` to `forget` (namespace-scoped if provided, dual-delete preserved if omitted, for the explicit /forget command) — across InMemory+File stores+the interface, with auto-extract's DELETE branch now passing kind through.
- **Why:** "tell it everything. It can't tell anyone." is a core promise — silently erasing something the user actually said is a direct violation (data integrity). Maximum diversification (after a date/grounding run, a fresh package @muse/memory, a fresh value-domain). Found by the scout in packages/memory (previously unscouted) + reproduced via dist.
- **Review points:** found by the scout → directly reproduced (dist: forget(u1,pet) wiped both facts.pet+preferences.pet) → RED (auto-extract cross-namespace + forget-kind) → fixed (both stores symmetrically + the interface's optional param + the auto-extract call site) → memory's 37 files green · cli/agent-core builds green (backward-compatible) · lint clean · added a File-store test (the production path). ④b Opus judge PASS 5/5 — #2 (a 26-check node truth table across both stores: undefined→both · fact→facts only · pref→prefs only, the File store's normalized-key resolution is also scoped) · #3 (a return-guard: forget(key,"fact") returns false+deletes nothing if only a pref exists) · #4 (no regression on the explicit /forget dual-delete + backward-compat) · #5 (auto-extract passes the correct kind, a mirror-case check).
- **Risk:** none — an optional argument (a 2-arg call/a 2-param mock is unaffected), the explicit /forget dual-delete is preserved, both stores are symmetric. LESSON: once classify knows the kind, it must consistently pass it through to *every* subsequent op (not just upsert but also delete) — omitting it causes cross-namespace damage. 2 runner-ups (a classify undefined-delete guard, an InMemory/Kysely vs. File normalization asymmetry) logged as backlog ◦s. Next: a different KIND/package. pnpm check's agent-core FAIL was concurrent-loop noise (unrelated).
